## 1. Strong Coupling In A Three-mode Superconducting-spin Hybrid Quantum System

- **Published**: 2026-06-25
- **Authors**: Yingqiu Mao, Han-Yu Ren, Zi-Yi Liu, Yi-Zheng Zhen, Tao Rong, Tao Jiang, Zhuo Chen, Zhe-Heng Yuan, Wen-Hua Qin, Xiaoran Zhang, Xiaobing Liu, Ming Gong, Kae Nemoto, William J. Munro, And Johannes Majer

- **Tags**: `spectroscopy`  `qubit`  `diamond`  `strong coupling`  `hybrid quantum system`

### TL;DR

Demonstrates strong coupling in a three-mode hybrid system of a superconducting transmon qubit, a coplanar-waveguide resonator, and an NV center ensemble.

### Motivation

Hybrid quantum systems combining superconducting qubits with spin ensembles are promising for quantum information processing, but achieving strong coupling in multi-mode configurations is challenging.

### Method

The authors fabricate a device with a fixed-frequency coplanar-waveguide resonator, a transmon qubit, and an NV center ensemble, and perform frequency-domain spectroscopy to observe avoided crossings.

### Result

They observe characteristic three-mode avoided crossings, indicating strong coupling between all three components, and extract coupling strengths and system parameters.

### Conclusion

This work demonstrates a viable platform for hybrid quantum systems with strong coupling, enabling future applications in quantum memory and transduction.

*Keywords: strong coupling, hybrid quantum system, superconducting qubit, NV center, circuit QED*

## 4. Learning Pure Quantum States Almost Without Regret

- **Score**: 8.5 / 10  |  **Recommended**: ✅ Yes
- **Source**: npj Quantum Info  |  **Feed**: npj Quantum Information
- **Direction**: Quantum Information
- **Published**: 2026-06-26
- **Authors**: Josep Lumbreras, Mikhail Terekhov, Marco Tomamichel
- **Link**: [https://www.nature.com/articles/s41534-026-01305-z](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41534-026-01305-z)
- **Tags**: `online learning`  `quantum state tomography`  `regret minimization`  `pure states`  `Bayesian inference`

### TL;DR

This paper develops a regret-minimizing online learning algorithm for pure quantum states, achieving near-optimal sample complexity.

### Motivation

Learning unknown quantum states efficiently is fundamental to quantum information processing, but existing methods often require many copies or assume prior knowledge. The authors aim to design an algorithm that learns pure states with minimal regret in an online setting.

### Method

The authors propose a Bayesian approach using a Dirichlet prior over the Bloch sphere and update beliefs via sequential measurements. They derive a regret bound by analyzing the information gain and using a novel concentration inequality for quantum measurements.

### Result

The algorithm achieves a regret of O(√T) for T rounds, matching the lower bound up to logarithmic factors, and outperforms previous methods in numerical simulations.

### Conclusion

The work provides a practical and theoretically optimal online learning algorithm for pure quantum states, with implications for quantum tomography and adaptive quantum metrology.

*Keywords: online learning, quantum state tomography, regret minimization, pure states, Bayesian inference*

### TL;DR

Experimental observation and modulation of the quantum Mpemba effect on a superconducting quantum processor.

### Motivation

The Mpemba effect, where a hotter system cools faster than a colder one, has been observed in classical systems; its quantum counterpart is of fundamental interest in non-equilibrium quantum thermodynamics.

### Method

Using an all-to-all connected, tunable-coupling superconducting processor, the authors prepare initial states at different effective temperatures and monitor their relaxation dynamics under controlled dissipation.

### Result

They observe the quantum Mpemba effect, where initially hotter states relax faster, and demonstrate modulation of the effect by tuning system parameters.

### Conclusion

The work provides experimental evidence of the quantum Mpemba effect, opening avenues for controlling relaxation dynamics in quantum systems and testing thermodynamic bounds.

The proposed measure offers a practical and computable way to quantify genuine multipartite entanglement, with potential for experimental verification.

## 12. Entanglement Entropy From Correlation Functions Of Scalar Fields In And Out Of Equilibrium

- **Score**: 8.5 / 10  |  **Recommended**: ✅ Yes
- **Source**: Phys. Rev. A  |  **Feed**: Physical Review A
- **Direction**: Quantum Information
- **Published**: 2026-06-26
- **Authors**: Mrinal Kanti Sarkar, Saranyo Moitra, And Rajdeep Sensarma
- **Link**: [http://link.aps.org/doi/10.1103/jt4g-j6mr](http://link.aps.org/doi/10.1103/jt4g-j6mr)
- **Tags**: `entanglement`  `Rényi entropy`  `entanglement entropy`  `replica trick`  `scalar field theory`

### TL;DR

The paper shows that odd-order Rényi entropies of interacting scalar fields can be computed as free energies of replicated systems with quadratic inter-replica couplings, enabling calculation from correlation functions.

### Motivation

Entanglement entropy is a key measure in quantum information and many-body physics, but computing it for interacting quantum field theories is challenging. The authors aim to provide a practical method using correlation functions.

### Method

They derive a replica trick for odd-order Rényi entropies, mapping the entropy to the free energy of a system with additional quadratic couplings between replicas in the subsystem. This allows calculation via two-point correlation functions.

### Result

The method is demonstrated for free scalar fields and interacting scalar fields in and out of equilibrium, showing that the entropy can be extracted from correlation functions without needing full state tomography.

### Conclusion

This work provides a new, computationally accessible way to compute entanglement entropy in quantum field theories, with potential applications to quantum information and non-equilibrium physics.

*Keywords: Rényi entropy, entanglement entropy, replica trick, scalar field theory, correlation functions*

### TL;DR

This paper generalizes Jarzynski's inequality to dissipative and field-theoretic systems, providing a weaker but more broadly applicable bound on nonequilibrium work.

### Motivation

The Jarzynski equality is a cornerstone of nonequilibrium thermodynamics but requires detailed knowledge of the work distribution; a simpler inequality could be more practical for complex systems.

### Method

The authors derive generalized Jarzynski inequalities using the concept of convexity and the Gibbs-Bogoliubov inequality, extending the result to systems with dissipation and field-theoretic descriptions.

### Result

They obtain a family of inequalities that bound the free energy difference in terms of the average work and a correction term depending on the system's dissipation or field configuration.

### Conclusion

The generalized inequalities offer a robust tool for estimating free energies in systems where the full Jarzynski equality is difficult to apply, with potential applications in quantum thermodynamics and statistical field theory.

*Keywords: Jarzynski inequality, nonequilibrium thermodynamics, free energy estimation, dissipative systems, field theory*

- **Tags**: `monitored quantum dynamics`  `quantum information lifetime`  `midcircuit measurements`  `scaling laws`  `quantum error correction`

### TL;DR

This paper discovers scaling laws for quantum information lifetime in monitored quantum dynamics, showing that midcircuit measurements can preserve quantum information for exponentially long times.

### Motivation

Understanding how quantum information decays in open systems is crucial for quantum computing; monitoring can potentially protect information.

### Method

The authors analyze monitored quantum circuits with random unitary gates and projective measurements, deriving scaling laws for the lifetime of quantum information as a function of system size and measurement rate.

### Result

They find that quantum information can survive for times exponential in system size when measurements are recorded, contrasting with the typical exponential decay without monitoring.

Critical quantum metrology can enhance precision but suffers from critical slowing down, limiting achievable precision in finite time.

### Method

Derives a fundamental bound on the quantum Fisher information per unit time using the quantum speed limit and critical exponents.

### Result

The precision scales as T^{-1/(zν)} where T is evolution time and z, ν are critical exponents, revealing a trade-off between precision and time.

### Conclusion

The bound is fundamental and can be saturated by optimal control, providing a guideline for designing critical metrology protocols.

*Keywords: critical quantum metrology, quantum speed limit, critical slowing down, quantum Fisher information, quantum phase transition*

### Motivation

Non-Gaussian states are essential for quantum advantage in continuous-variable quantum information, but a unified measure of non-Gaussianity was lacking. The symplectic rank fills this gap by providing an operationally meaningful resource measure.

### Result

The symplectic rank is shown to be a monotone under Gaussian operations, and it provides tight lower bounds on the number of non-Gaussian resources needed for tasks like state compression and tomography.

### Conclusion

The symplectic rank is a powerful new tool for quantifying non-Gaussianity, with implications for resource theories and practical quantum information processing in continuous-variable systems.

### Motivation

To understand the thermal melting of vortex lattices in two-dimensional Bose gases, which is a fundamental many-body phenomenon, and to extend recent experimental results.

### Method

The stochastic projected Gross–Pitaevskii equation is used to simulate finite-temperature equilibrium states in a harmonic plus quartic trap, extracting vortex configurations from density snapshots.

### Result

Clear signatures of the two-step Kosterlitz–Thouless–Halperin–Nelson–Young melting scenario are identified, with finite-size effects significantly affecting defect proliferation at lower rotation frequencies.

### Conclusion

The simulations provide detailed characterization of crystalline, hexatic, and liquid phases, highlighting the importance of finite-size effects in vortex lattice melting.

To provide a rigorous derivation of the modified Langevin noise formalism (MLNF) from canonical quantization of macroscopic electromagnetism (CQME) by explicitly expressing polariton operators in terms of canonical field operators.

### Conclusion

- **Tags**: `dynamics`  `simulation`  `ultrafast photoionization`  `Wigner delay`  `causality`

### TL;DR

This paper studies ultrafast photoionization dynamics in argon at the Amusia-Cooper minimum using ab initio simulations, focusing on causality and Wigner delays.

### Motivation

To understand the role of causality in ultrafast photoionization and resolve gauge-dependent Wigner delays that appear to violate causality.

### Method

Relativistic time-dependent configuration-interaction singles (RTDCIS) simulations in length and velocity gauges, combined with a simple analytical model and Wigner distribution analysis.

### Result

Length gauge yields positive Wigner delays while velocity gauge yields negative delays of several femtoseconds, but numerical wave-packet simulations confirm causal behavior in both gauges.

### Conclusion

Weighted delays constructed from the Wigner distribution reconcile the gauge discrepancy, and spin-orbit coupling effects are significant; Schrödinger kitten and cat states emerge at the ACM.

*Keywords: ultrafast photoionization, Wigner delay, causality, Amusia-Cooper minimum, RTDCIS*

To understand how uniaxial stress can tune magnetic frustration and induce new magnetic phases in frustrated magnets.

Combined x-ray diffraction, stress-strain measurements, and neutron diffraction under uniaxial stress.

### TL;DR

This paper presents a high-stability optical clock based on a continuously ground-state cooled Al+ ion, achieving reduced averaging time without compromising accuracy.

### Motivation

Single-ion optical clocks require long averaging times to reach statistical uncertainty, limiting their practical use. The authors aim to reduce this time by extending probe times while mitigating the associated time-dilation shift.

### Method

They implement continuous ground-state cooling of an Al+ ion using a sympathetic cooling scheme, allowing longer probe durations. They characterize and correct for the time-dilation shift due to excess micromotion and other effects.

### Result

The clock achieves a fractional frequency instability of 1.5e-15 at 1 second and reaches a statistical uncertainty of 1e-18 in under 1000 seconds, while maintaining systematic uncertainty below 1e-18.

### Conclusion

Continuous ground-state cooling enables high-stability operation of a single-ion optical clock without sacrificing accuracy, making it more practical for applications requiring rapid measurements.

*Keywords: optical clock, aluminum ion, ground-state cooling, frequency stability, time-dilation shift*

### Method

The authors present experimental results from the Wendelstein 7-X stellarator, showing stable peaked density profiles and high confinement achieved through careful optimization of magnetic geometry and operational parameters.

### Result

The triple product achieved in W7-X is comparable to that of tokamaks, marking a significant milestone for stellarator performance.

### Conclusion

Optimized stellarators can achieve tokamak-level performance, supporting their viability as fusion reactor candidates.

### TL;DR

This paper studies first-passage times of swimming microorganisms near a wall using numerical simulations, focusing on hydrodynamic attraction and hindered diffusion.

### Motivation

Understanding how microorganisms navigate near surfaces is important for biological and ecological processes, but the role of hydrodynamic interactions and diffusion on first-passage times is not well understood.

### Method

Numerical simulations of force-dipole microswimmers near a no-slip wall are performed to compute first-passage-time statistics.

### Result

Wall-induced hindered diffusion delays wall encounters, while active hydrodynamic attraction leads to distinct behaviors for pushers and pullers, including a nonmonotonic dependence on dipole strength.

### Conclusion

Hydrodynamic interactions and diffusion significantly affect the first-passage times of swimming microorganisms near walls, with implications for microbial ecology and biophysics.

*Keywords: microswimmers, hydrodynamic interactions, first-passage time, hindered diffusion, active matter*

To test the cosmological principle by comparing the dipole anisotropy in the matter distribution with the CMB dipole, which could reveal a fundamental inconsistency in the standard model of cosmology.

### Method

The authors analyze recent catalogs of radio galaxies and quasars, comparing the observed dipole in their distribution with the CMB dipole, and review potential biases and systematic effects.

### Result

A significant inconsistency is found between the matter dipole and the CMB dipole, suggesting a possible violation of the cosmological principle.

### Conclusion

The cosmic dipole anomaly may challenge the ΛCDM model, but further investigation is needed to rule out systematic errors or alternative interpretations.

*Keywords: cosmic dipole, cosmological principle, ΛCDM, large-scale structure, CMB*

## 460. Deterministic Initiation Of Inner-shell X-ray Lasers By Two-color Raman Driving

- **Score**: 0.0 / 10  |  **Recommended**: ❌ No
- **Source**: New J. Phys.  |  **Feed**: New Journal of Physics
- **Direction**: General / Other
- **Published**: 2026-06-30
- **Authors**: Carles Serrat
- **Link**: [https://iopscience.iop.org/article/10.1088/1367-2630/ae7f43](https://iopscience.iop.org/article/10.1088/1367-2630/ae7f43)
- **Tags**: `led`  `polarization`  `propagation`  `dynamics`  `emission`

### TL;DR

This paper proposes a deterministic seeding mechanism for inner-shell x-ray lasers using two-color Raman driving, shifting the lasing onset from stochastic noise to a driven coherence.

### Motivation

Conventional inner-shell x-ray lasers are initiated by amplified spontaneous emission (ASE), leading to stochastic temporal onset and phase. The authors aim to achieve deterministic control over the lasing initiation.

### Method

The authors analyze a regime where a resonant two-color Raman drive induces a core-valence coherence at the lasing frequency, acting as a seed. They use a microscopic density-matrix description coupled to a linear Maxwell-Bloch-Langevin propagation model with calibrated spontaneous-emission noise, and derive an analytical criterion separating noise-dominated ASE from coherence-dominated seeded regime.

### Result

An explicit analytical criterion is derived, depending on Raman drive amplitude, small-signal gain, gate duration, and noise strength. Numerical integration confirms the predicted crossover. A microscopic evaluation for Ne provides a quantitative mapping to pump intensities.

### Conclusion

The mechanism identifies a coherence-dominated operating regime for inner-shell x-ray lasing, shifting the field origin from spontaneous fluctuations to a driven microscopic coherence, while gain dynamics remain unchanged.

*Keywords: inner-shell x-ray laser, Raman driving, coherence seeding, amplified spontaneous emission, Maxwell-Bloch-Langevin model*

### TL;DR

This paper reports an ATLAS search for Higgs boson decays into pseudoscalars decaying to tau leptons, with no significant excess over the Standard Model.

### Motivation

To probe exotic decays of the Higgs boson beyond the Standard Model, specifically into four tau leptons via intermediate pseudoscalars.

### Method

Analyzes 140 fb^-1 of proton-proton collision data at 13 TeV from the ATLAS detector, selecting events with one or two hadronically decaying taus and the remaining taus decaying to electrons or muons.

### Result

No significant excess is observed; upper limits on the branching ratio are set between 0.06 and 0.23 for pseudoscalar masses from 15 to 60 GeV.

### Conclusion

The results constrain models predicting exotic Higgs decays, but do not show evidence for new physics.

*Keywords: Higgs boson, exotic decays, pseudoscalar, tau leptons, ATLAS*

## 463. Orthogonal Phase Modulation And Lissajous Mode Decoupling In Light-induced Thermoelastic Spectroscopy For Real-time Multi-component Gas Sensing

- **Score**: 0.0 / 10  |  **Recommended**: ❌ No

- **Direction**: General / Other
- **Published**: 2026-06-18
- **Authors**: Hanxu Ma, Shunda Qiao, Ying He, Haiyue Sun And Yufei Ma
- **Link**: [https://iopscience.iop.org/article/10.1088/1361-6633/ae79a3](https://iopscience.iop.org/article/10.1088/1361-6633/ae79a3)
- **Tags**: `led`  `spectroscopy`  `feedback`  `absorption`  `crosstalk`

### TL;DR

This paper presents a novel optical gas sensing technique using orthogonal phase modulation and Lissajous mode decoupling in light-induced thermoelastic spectroscopy for real-time multi-component gas detection.

### Motivation

Real-time multi-component gas sensing is important for environmental monitoring and industrial safety, but existing methods suffer from channel crosstalk and complexity.

### Method

The authors use two continuous-wave lasers modulated at the same frequency with a 45° phase difference, co-exciting a quartz tuning fork. The piezoelectric signal is orthogonally demodulated to decouple the two gas concentration signals.

### Result

The sensor achieves linear responses for methane and acetylene with low systematic errors (0.54% and 0.79%) and detection limits of 0.32 ppm and 0.29 ppm, respectively.

### Conclusion

The proposed OPM-LITES sensor provides a simple and efficient solution for real-time multi-component gas detection with a novel theoretical framework for mode decoupling.

*Keywords: orthogonal phase modulation, Lissajous mode decoupling, light-induced thermoelastic spectroscopy, quartz tuning fork, multi-component gas sensing*

Conventional methods for studying nanoparticle-protein interactions disrupt native protein coronas and fail to capture dynamic intracellular trafficking, limiting the understanding of nanomedicine delivery.

The authors developed a nanozyme-based proximity labeling strategy that covalently tags proteins in the vicinity of nanoparticles within living cells, enabling in situ mapping of the protein corona and trafficking pathways.

The method successfully identified subcellular interactomes and revealed trafficking pathways of nanoparticles, providing insights into their intracellular fate.

### Method

The authors develop a mathematical model to simulate tsetse population dynamics and control strategies, validated with field data from Chad.

### TL;DR

This paper presents a method for intracellular delivery of full-length antibodies using organ-targeted lipid nanoparticles.

### Motivation

Antibodies are highly selective therapeutics but cannot cross cell membranes, limiting their use to extracellular targets. Many disease-relevant proteins are intracellular, so a delivery method is needed.

### Method

The authors developed lipid nanoparticles (LNPs) functionalized with targeting ligands to deliver full-length antibodies into specific cell types in vivo.

### Result

The LNPs successfully delivered functional antibodies into target cells in animal models, enabling intracellular targeting of proteins such as transcription factors.

### Conclusion

Organ-targeted LNPs provide a viable platform for intracellular antibody delivery, expanding the therapeutic potential of antibodies to intracellular targets.
